[[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] According to this study, Austria is in second place among 135 countries under investigation. They require efficient approval procedures and warn against increasing labour costs. There will be no repayment of holidays already in use, with the exception of redundancies and unwarranted retirement, but you will probably recognise that this is appropriate and justified in this case. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] In connection with this, ladies and gentlemen, we want to take a very essential step and clean up with an anachronism that exists only in Austria, namely, the difference between workers and employees. It is a question of social justice at work, of equalizing workers and employees, and I hope that you will at least go along with it, ladies and gentlemen, of the opposition, because that was a decades-old demand of the ÖGB, Mr Vergestnitsch, and I am sure that the fulfilment of this request will come to your agreement by the new Federal Government. ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00012_d7e17916	We're now on the 3rd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Jäger has spoken. Voluntary speaking time limit: 8 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00012_d7e17943	Mr President. Minister! Mr State Secretary! Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! There is no doubt that the Armenian genocide between 1915 and 1917 was among the most disgusting crimes in the 20th century. ==References== For example, the denial of this genocide, the distortion of facts by many Turkish governments to this day. That is why I welcome the present proposal, i.e. the treatment of this issue in the Human Rights Committee – alone because at the time Austria-Hungary as an ally state had strong historical relations with Turkey. However, I do not want to prejudge the human rights committee's treatment, which is the recognition within the meaning of the Convention on the Prevention and Punishment of the Crimes of the Genocide, adopted by the UN General Assembly on 9 May. ==References== I think that we in the Human Rights Committee should talk exactly and thoroughly about how useful it is to equate various types of genocide that are very long behind, according to this Convention, which was decided after the Second World War in the face of the killing of so many Jewish people. However, I would like to say that it is very unfortunate that Turkey is still running a very backward and too high-voltage minority policy. I would like to add this to the example of the Kurds. They are forbidden by their own language and culture. I think that Europe's maturity – in this respect Turkey must be taken into account – includes a modern minority law and a proper relationship to historical truths and to the historical past. In this context, I would like to mention that Turkey has ultimately gone on to postpone the execution of the Kurdish leader Öcalan. This has led to a recognition of the European Court of Justice and has dealt with this issue properly. I would like to comment briefly on the Armenian genocide. The Armenians had a majority in several Ottoman provinces at the time, i.e. at the beginning of the last century, and there was the regime of the young Turks who were allied to Austria-Hungary and Germany in the First World War. After Turkey entered the war there was an invasion into the Caucasus. The very badly prepared Turkish troops were defeated by the war-orientated and better ordered Russians in January 1915 at Sarikamis. The disorderly retreat of survivors through Armenian territory. In the end, both officers and soldiers blamed the Armenians for the defeat that had obviously been caused by strategic errors. Contrary to all the facts, the young Turks, the then Turkish regime, were convinced that the Armenians were a deadly threat to Pan-Turcism. The First World War provided them with a favorable opportunity to deal with this inner enemy. The destruction of the Armenians, their "destruction with the roots", as Hannah Arendt said, meant liquidations, deportations, massacres, resettlement marches through desert areas to concentration camps in the deserts of Mesopotamia. They led to a rapid destruction of the Armenians. Women, children, old people were sent to death marches, two thirds of the Armenians of the Ottoman Empire were murdered within two years. They speak of about 1.5 million people who died at the time. However, the Armenian genocide was not a response to Armenian provocations, as Turkish governments subsequently represented, but it was a response to the military disasters of the First World War and, of course, a step within the Turkish national revolution. I think we should take a closer look at this issue in the Human Rights Committee. I have already mentioned this: Austria was then allied with Turkey. It's about these commemorative days, too. ==References====External links==* Official website Let's go. I think it is necessary that we can truly deal with our history. We all know that only those peoples who are honest with their history have a democratic and honest future. I believe that we should also accept this issue in the current situation. Thank you.

I would like to stress the word "current" because it is known that no proper budgetary procedure has yet been carried out. We must make up for this in the fast-seated procedure so that we can put forward a budget for 2000. Based on the current figures, we do not have a gross deficit of 109 billion schilling, but of about 270 billion schilling! [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] Of these 270 billion schillings, about 160 billion schillings are deducted for the annual repayments of the financial debt. This results in a net deficit of 109 billion schilling. [[Abg. Aumayr: They don't know what gross and what net is!]] The need for consolidation after Maastricht is 62 billion schilling, that is, from 109 billion schilling to 62 billion schilling, which is about 47 billion schilling. It is not easy, however, for the surpluses to be deducted from the various funds and then there is only a need for consolidation of 20 or 25 billion schilling, not because this deduction is not fully Maastricht-relevant. According to our calculations, at best 13 billion Schilling Maastricht-compliant can be used for this. It has already been said that there is a need for a legislative budget procedure, because neither formal nor content has been provided for the 2000 budget, either by appropriate budget accompanying laws or, above all, by a round of surveys in the individual departments. All of this has to be recovered shortly, even though the new Federal Government only took place on 4 May. ==References== [[Abg. Dr. Antoni: But that's right!]] ==References====External links== ==References====External links== However, it cannot be used as a basis for the year, as has been said today, on the basis of the 1999 success figures. Rather, there is a need for a budget of its own for the year 2000 because the need for consolidation is higher compared with the previous year. In addition, certain legal measures have been taken which lead to additional expenditure. For example, there has been a pay round for the public service, which costs about 3.2 billion schilling. It is therefore necessary to provide for this by means of a law of its own. Thank you.

Now the report has come to an end that the war has ended, that Russia has won and that President Putin is also using a human rights representative in Chechnya. I think that this issue will continue to concern us for a very long time. On the other hand – I think that we should also deal with this – this war in Chechnya is, of course, not only a war of the Russians against terrorists in this area. I have here an article by the alternative Nobel Prize winner Hermann Scheer, who claims that the Chechen conflict is an escalating resource war. It must be noted that the Russian government is very likely that the oil pipelines are running through Chechnya and that a new pipeline is now being built in the Caucasus area, which will pass through Turkey and receive support from the USA. This was also discussed at the OSCE Conference in Istanbul. You have to see what problems this can lead to with Russia. If Russia is completely cut off from this oil-rich region in the South Caucasus, then this will lead to increased conflicts, to increased conflicts. I think that there will be a very difficult situation both in terms of democratic development in Russia and in the European Union's cooperation with Russia. We have already discussed the situation before the First World War today, when, precisely from this southern area of the Caucasus, there have been major conflicts that ended in a World War. I think we should take the situation that we find there very seriously. I believe that the Western world must be aware of the extent to which the Russians should also be involved in the oil deposits. Again, back to the human rights situation: I hope that now, after the end of the war, it is possible that Russia will allow foreign aid. The first UNHCR transport in Grosny has arrived, but it would be necessary to make foreign aid available to a greater extent there. I would have another question to the Foreign Minister. The OSCE has undertaken to provide assistance, particularly in Chechnya. I am concerned about the extent to which you can actually carry out your tasks in the OSCE, as there are so many other problems with the Austrian government. I would like to ask you about this. There is a criticism of Austria's OSCE Presidency – perhaps you would like to comment on this – and it is reported from Norway that there are diplomats from several countries expressing their criticism of Austria's OSCE Presidency and that you are finally being asked to start preparing the elections in Kosovo. In view of this global political situation, I think the OSCE is more required than ever. I hope that Austria can really carry out these tasks.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00012_d7e23465	Dear ladies! High house! Of course, we take seriously the protection of the health of the population from electromagnetic rays – that is the background of this Green request. We also know that there are fears and concerns in this area in the population, and of course we have to deal with these fears. We do not see our duty to startle fears and make politics with them, but to reduce fears! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We do not want to stir up emotions in this context, but to gain rational and scientific knowledge and then implement it politically and objectively. That is our task, so at least we see our responsibility as a government party! Under the current rules, the operation of GSM transmitters is subject to an operating authorisation from the competent telecommunications office. As part of this operating permit, it is of course also necessary to take into account the protection of human life and health. The parameters to be used are derived from the ÖNORM S 1120, which contains the currently valid limit values. These limit values are also in line with the recommendations of the EU Council of 12 March. In July 1999, we agreed to a recent agreement. The rules currently in force are therefore also in line with the directives of the European Union. However, we know that there has been a discussion about health hazards through electromagnetic waves for a long time. That's why we came to the National Council on 14 November. In July 1999, the resolution adopted a study on the risk posed by radio frequency electromagnetic fields. – This study was conducted by Professor Dr. Jiři Silni from the Research Centre for Electromagnetic Environmental Impact at the Rheinisch-Westfälische Technische Hochschule Aachen, which is an internationally renowned expert in this field. Seven independent measurements were carried out in Austria, of course with modern measuring equipment: The measured maximum power density values in the study are much lower than those set by the ÖNORM. It has also been noted that, according to ÖNORM, large safety distances are used and the limit values are set in such a way that minor warming of the body is excluded by more than 0.1 degrees. The ÖNORM is ultimately certified that it is in line with the latest international findings and that, with its current level of knowledge limits, it protects the population sufficiently from possible effects of exposure by radio frequency fields. – Ladies and gentlemen! You can visit and study this study on the Internet! Ladies and gentlemen! These are the facts that are not scientifically objectionable! Mrs Colleague, you should tell the population that scientific studies by Austrian ÖNORM have confirmed that it is in line with the latest scientific findings! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] In your application, you require a four thousand and five hundredths of the limit set by the WHO and the EU Council recommendation mentioned above. Ladies and gentlemen! In any event, proper operation of mobile phones under economically reasonable conditions is certainly no longer possible under the conditions of your application! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Your request also lacks any scientific justification for the need for the limit value presented by you! . We are ready for discussion, but we call for a clarification of the discussion. Because in view of the actual health risk, the constant fear you are doing is in fact grossly responsible! For science also has to admit that research has not been able to present any solid evidence to date, whether and what risks to human health actually arise from the radiation electromagnetic fields by GSM. Ladies and gentlemen! I think it is extremely double-bedded if the representatives of the Greens keep walking around the ear with the phone, but at the same time they are afraid of the radiation and want to make political change!
